In fact, everything feels far too real and overwhelming for me right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you been able to sleep at all since the accident, or are you having trouble falling asleep or staying asleep because of the fear and replaying images?\n\nPatient: I'm having terrible trouble sleeping, doctor. I lie awake staring at the ceiling for hours, and when I finally nod off, I jerk awake with my heart racing and those awful images of the street fair in my head. I just never feel like I’ve really slept.\n\nDoctor: Did these symptoms start within minutes to hours after the accident, or did they begin later that day or the next day?\n\nPatient: They didn't start immediately in the minutes right after it happened. They really began developing in the days right after the accident, and it has been going on like this for about a week now.\n\nDoctor: Have these symptoms been present for less than one month, or have they continued for longer than a month?\n\nPatient: Yes, it has been much less than a month. The accident only happened last week, so I've been dealing with all of this for just about seven days now.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any period of feeling completely unable to remember what happened during the accident, or do you remember it clearly?\n\nPatient: Yes, I actually have some big blank spots in my memory from that day. I can remember setting up the booth, and then suddenly I was holding someone’s hand on the ground, but I can't piece together what happened in between. No matter how hard I try, I just can't remember how I got from one moment to the next.\n\nDoctor: Have these symptoms lasted less than one month since the event, or have they continued for more than a month?\n\nPatient: It has definitely been less than a month. The accident happened just recently, and these feelings and reactions started right after and have only been going on for about a week now.\n\nDoctor: Have your symptoms been improving at all, or have they stayed severe for more than a month since the accident?"

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
No, they haven't improved at all, but it hasn't been a month yet since the accident only happened last week. For this past week, these feelings have stayed completely intense and constant without any real relief.

No matter how hard I try, I just can't remember how I got from one moment to the next.\n\nDoctor: Have these symptoms lasted less than one month since the event, or have they continued for more than a month?\n\nPatient: It has definitely been less than a month. The accident happened just recently, and these feelings and reactions started right after and have only been going on for about a week now.\n\nDoctor: Have your symptoms been improving at all, or have they stayed severe for more than a month since the accident?\n\nPatient: No, they haven't improved at all, but it hasn't been a month yet since the accident only happened last week. Subtypes: Failure to Control, Intrusive Worry, Chaining of Worry\n[S058] Restlessness_Tension: Restlessness, or feeling keyed up or on edge. Subtypes: Cleaning or Washing Compulsions, Checking Compulsions, Ordering and Arranging Compulsions, Mental Acts, Repeating Rituals\n[S067] Preoccupation_with_Appearance: Preoccupation with one or more perceived defects or flaws in physical appearance that are not observable or appear slight to others. Subtypes: Intrusive Memories, Distressing Dreams, Dissociative Reactions (Flashbacks), Psychological Distress at Cues, Physiological Reactions to Cues\n[S070] Trauma_Avoidance: Persistent avoidance of stimuli associated with the traumatic event. Subtypes: Self-Blame, Blaming Others\n[S074] Persistent_Negative_Emotional_State: Persistent negative emotional state (e.g., fear, horror, anger, guilt, or shame).  Subtypes: Persistent Fear, Persistent Horror, Persistent Anger, Persistent Guilt, Persistent Shame\n[S075] Social_Detachment: Feelings of detachment or estrangement from others.  Subtypes: Emotional Detachment, Social Isolation, Difficulty with Intimacy, Loss of Belonging\n[S076] Inability_Positive_Emotions: Persistent inability to experience positive emotions (e.g., inability to experience happiness, satisfaction, or loving feelings).  Subtypes: Anhedonia for Happiness, Lack of Satisfaction, Inability to Feel Love, Emotional Numbness\n[S077] Irritability_Aggression: Irritable behavior and angry outbursts (with little or no provocation) typically expressed as verbal or physical aggression.  Subtypes: Impulsive Anger, Verbal Aggression, Physical Aggression, Chronic Irritability\n[S078] Reckless_Self_Destructive_Behavior: Reckless or self-destructive behavior pattern that endangers one's safety or health. Subtypes: With Depressed Mood, With Anxiety, With Mixed Anxiety and Depressed Mood, With Disturbance of Conduct, With Mixed Disturbance of Emotions and Conduct, Unspecified\n[S082] Depersonalization_Derealization: Altered sense of the reality of one's self or surroundings after a traumatic event. Subtypes: Failure to Control, Intrusive Worry, Chaining of Worry\n[S058] Restlessness_Tension: Restlessness, or feeling keyed up or on edge.
